Just a handful of this or that because I don't really have the drive to make a real meal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have less interest in food than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. Food just doesn't... it doesn't hold any appeal for me right now. Like I said, nothing really tastes right, so I just don't care about it the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your eating or not eating is related to how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose it is. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling. It's like... like the color has just gone out of the day before I even get dressed. When I feel that down, I just don't care enough to eat.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or worried about gaining weight when you do eat?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really worry about that. Weight hasn't been on my mind at all, honestly. It's just that the food itself doesn't seem worth the effort.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food is related to anything besides your low mood?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, it's just the sadness, really. Like I said, things just don't taste right, and I don't have the energy for it. I haven't noticed anything else causing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating issues started after your low mood began?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. It's a little hard to remember exactly which came first. They just sort of... go together now. I haven't really kept track of the exact timing of it all, so I couldn't say for certain.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or empty most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Most mornings, I wake up with this heavy, low feeling. It's... well, it's just like the color has already gone out of the day before I even get dressed.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low mood?\n\nPatient: I'd say this low, colorless feeling has been with me for about a year and two months now. It's just been a steady stretch for that long.\n\nDoctor: Has your changed eating pattern caused any problems in your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, it has. I used to teach craft classes, but I've stopped doing that entirely. And I find myself missing phone calls, appointments, and little family tasks that I used to handle without a second thought. I'm still managing to live on my own in my house, but I definitely rely a lot more on reminders now, and I just put off things that used to be a normal part of my routine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in the way your clothes fit you over the past year?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, they are fitting a bit differently. Actually, that's what made me come in to see someone. My eating has gotten all turned around lately. Some days I just completely forget to eat lunch. But then other days, I'll find myself just standing at the kitchen counter, eating crackers and sweets... even though nothing really tastes right to me anymore. It's just very irregular now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: I haven't stepped on a scale to know the exact number of pounds, to be honest. But like I was just saying a moment ago, my eating has just gotten completely turned around. Because I forget lunch entirely some days, and then other days I'm just standing at the counter eating sweets and crackers—even though nothing tastes right to me. So I'm just not eating proper, regular meals anymore, and I'm sure that's why my weight has dropped.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your weight loss over the past year has been noticeable to others?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose so, since my clothes are fitting differently. But really, like I said, the main thing that brought me in is just how mixed up my eating has gotten. Some days I just don't eat lunch at all, I completely forget. And then other times I'm just standing there at the counter, eating sweets and crackers, even though food doesn't even taste right to me anymore. It's just all turned around.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would start eating normally again if your low mood improved?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure about that. I haven't thought about it that way. I just know that right now, my appetite is completely off and nothing seems to appeal to me. I suppose it's possible things might get better if I wasn't feeling so down, but I just don't know for certain.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
